Given $B_n$, adjoin $T_{\leq q}b$ for all $b\in B_n$ and all $q\in\Q$,
then take its real closure inside the real closed field $H$ to obtain
$B_{n+1}$. Each step is countable: there are countably many truncations and
countably many elements algebraic over a countable field.
The union $B=\bigcup_nB_n$ is a countable field, is truncation-closed, and
is real closed. For the last assertion, any finite list of polynomial
coefficients and any positive element occur at one stage, and the requisite
odd-degree roots and positive square roots occur at the next stage.

The field $C=\overline B^{\,v}$ is complete and separable, hence admissibly
Polish by the same argument as Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:hulls}.
For $f\in C$ and $q\in\Q$, choose $b\in B$ with $v(f-b)>q$.
Then $T_{\leq q}f=T_{\leq q}b\in B$, proving \eqref{pma:ser:eq:trunccore}.
To prove real closedness, regard $B(i)$ inside $H(i)$.
The coefficientwise valuation satisfies
\[
 v(a+ib)=\min\{v(a),v(b)\}\qquad(a,b\in H).
\]
Consequently its completion is $C(i)$. Since $B$ is real closed, $B(i)$ is
algebraically closed; Lemma~\ref{pma:ser:lem:completionac} makes $C(i)$ algebraically
closed. As $C$ is ordered, it is real closed.
\end{proof}

\subsection{An exact global invariant}

Let $\operatorname{pcov}_k(H)$ be the least cardinality of a family of
admissibly Polish intermediate subfields $k(t^\Q)\subseteq F\subseteq H$
whose union is $H$. The topology on a covering field need not initially be
the inherited valuation topology.

\begin{theorem}[The Polish field-cover number]\label{pma:ser:thm:covernumber}
For every countable ordered coefficient field $k$,
\[
 \operatorname{pcov}_k\bigl(k((t^\Q))\bigr)=2^{\aleph_0}.
\]
For $k=\ka$, the same minimum holds even if all covering fields are required
to be real closed, truncation-closed, and Polish in their valuation topology.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For the upper bound, $|H|=2^{\aleph_0}$, and the fields $F_{\{h\}}$ from
Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:hulls}, one for each $h\in H$, cover $H$.
For real algebraic coefficients use the stronger hulls from
Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:rchull} instead.

For the lower bound, use the continuum-sized binary family
\[
 h_b=\sum_{n\geq0}b(n)t^{1-1/(n+2)},\qquad b\in2^\N.
\]
Distinct centers differ in absolute ordered value by more than $2t$.
In any one admissibly Polish intermediate field, the intervals of radius
$t$ about the centers it contains are nonempty, open, and pairwise disjoint.
It therefore contains only countably many members of this binary family.
A union of $\kappa<2^{\aleph_0}$ such fields contains at most
$\kappa\cdot\aleph_0<2^{\aleph_0}$ members of the family, and cannot cover
$H$. This cardinal calculation is in ZFC and uses no continuum hypothesis.
\end{proof}

The local and global statements now fit together exactly. Every countable
collection of Hahn data is contained in one well-behaved Polish field;
no such field is maximal; nevertheless continuum many are necessary to
cover all Hahn data. These conclusions are not consequences of the mere
cardinality $|H|=2^{\aleph_0}$: the lower bound uses the fixed-scale
separation forced by arithmetic and order.

\section{A countable omnific integer part and its exact floor regularity}
\label{pma:ser:sec:floor}

Throughout this section $k=\ka\subseteq\R$. Define
\begin{equation}\label{pma:ser:eq:integerpart}
 \Ik=\Z\oplus\bigoplus_{q\in\Q_{<0}}kt^q.
\end{equation}
Thus an element has finitely many negative-exponent terms and an ordinary
integer constant term. The direct sum is literal finite support; replacing it
by arbitrary Hahn support would change the ring.

\begin{theorem}[A common integer part]\label{pma:ser:thm:commonip}
The ring $\Ik$ is countable and discretely ordered. It is an integer part of
both $\Pk$ and $\Lk$. If
\[
 f=p+c+u,
\]
where $p$ is the negative principal part, $c=f_0\in k$, and $u$ has only
positive exponents, then
\begin{equation}\label{pma:ser:eq:floor}
 \floorI{f}
 =p+\lfloor c\rfloor-\begin{cases}
  1,&c\in\Z\text{ and }u<0,\\
  0,&\text{otherwise}.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Finite rational supports with coefficients in a countable field form a
countable set. Addition and multiplication preserve finite negative support
and integer constant terms, so $\Ik$ is a countable unital subring.
If a nonzero element has a negative leading exponent, its absolute ordered
value exceeds every positive ordinary integer. Otherwise it is a nonzero
ordinary integer. Hence $1$ is its least positive element.

For $f\in\Lk$, left-finiteness makes $p$ finite, and every nonzero $u$ is
smaller in absolute ordered value than every positive constant of $k$.
If $c\notin\Z$, both $c-\lfloor c\rfloor$ and
$\lfloor c\rfloor+1-c$ are positive constants; the infinitesimal $u$
cannot change either inequality. If $c\in\Z$ and $u\geq0$, the floor is
$p+c$. If $c\in\Z$ and $u<0$, the floor is $p+c-1$.
In every case the stated element $a$ satisfies $a\leq f<a+1$.
Uniqueness follows because distinct elements of $\Ik$ differ by at least
$1$ in absolute ordered value. Since $\Pk\subseteq\Lk$ and contains
$\Ik$, the same result holds in $\Pk$.
\end{proof}

\noindent\writetag{} With $t^q\mapsto\omega^{-q}$, formula \eqref{pma:ser:eq:floor}
is the collection's floor rule \replabel{odg:thm:floor}, proved in Lean for
every surreal, and source~03's \cref{pma:bor:lem:floor}. For $k=\ka$, the
ring $\Ik$ is source~03's finite principal-part ring $\A$ with coefficients
restricted to $\ka$ (with $X=t^{-1}$), a countable subring of it; it is also
the Shepherdson ring used by batch~86's manuscript~10 in
\fn{Algebra/SurrealNumbers/docs/surreal/discrete-initial-subgroups-and-omnific-normalization}.

Formula~\eqref{pma:ser:eq:floor}, particularly its correction for a negative
infinitesimal at an integer constant, is a specialization of the omnific
normal-form floor rule already documented in ProveIt \cite{s09:ProveIt}.
It is not claimed as a new floor formula. The following topological
consequences are the point of its use here.

\begin{theorem}[Exact discontinuity set and Baire class]
\label{pma:ser:thm:floorregularity}
Give $\Lk$ its unique admissible Polish topology and $\Ik$ the discrete
topology. The floor map $\floorI{\cdot}:\Lk\to\Ik$ is Baire class one.
Its set of discontinuities is exactly $\Ik$, a countable closed nowhere-dense
subset of $\Lk$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Its fibers are the half-open intervals $[a,a+1)$ for $a\in\Ik$.
If $f\notin\Ik$ and $a=\floorI f$, then $a<f<a+1$, so a sufficiently
small order neighborhood of $f$ lies in the same fiber. The floor is
continuous there. At $a\in\Ik$, the sequence
$a-t^n$ converges to $a$, while
\[
 \floorI{a-t^n}=a-1\ne a=\floorI a.
\]
Thus the discontinuity set is exactly $\Ik$.

For $n\geq0$ let $T_n f=\sum_{q\leq n}f_qt^q$, a finite polynomial.
The map $T_n$ into its discrete, countable range is locally constant:
$v(f-g)>n$ implies $T_nf=T_ng$.
Consequently
\[
 F_n(f)=\floorI{T_nf}
\]
is a continuous map $\Lk\to\Ik$. These maps converge pointwise to the
floor, and indeed are eventually equal to it at each input. The negative
principal part and constant are already fixed for all $n\geq0$.
When the constant is an integer and the positive tail is nonzero, the sign
needed for the correction is determined by the first positive exponent,
which is included once $n$ is large enough. In all other cases no such
correction can change. This proves the Baire class one assertion.

Closed discreteness follows from Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:integerparts}. Its interior
is empty because every neighborhood of $a\in\Ik$ contains
$a+t^n\notin\Ik$ for large $n$. Hence it is nowhere dense.
\end{proof}

\begin{example}[Boundary tests]
The formulas include
\[
 \floorI{-t}=-1,\qquad
 \floorI{7-t^{1/3}}=6,\qquad
 \floorI{7+t^{1/3}}=7,
\]
and
\[
 \floorI{3t^{-2}-5t^{-1/2}+\tfrac32-t^{10}}
   =3t^{-2}-5t^{-1/2}+1.
\]
A finite-precision procedure that ignores a small negative tail at an integer
constant is wrong exactly at the kind of boundary detected by the theorem.
The continuous approximants above permit eventual correction; they do not
provide an input-uniform stage at which correction is finished.
\end{example}

\subsection{Floors in the larger Polish hulls}

The strengthened hulls of Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:rchull} support the same regularity
without restricting their negative principal parts to finite support.

\begin{theorem}[Canonical integer parts in real closed Polish hulls]
\label{pma:ser:thm:hullfloor}
Let $B\subseteq C\subseteq\ka((t^\Q))$ be as in
Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:rchull}. Then
\[
 J_C=\{f\in C:\supp f\subseteq\Q_{\leq0},\ f_0\in\Z\}
\]
is a countable closed discrete integer part of $C$, and $J_C\subseteq B$.
Its floor is Baire class one as a map into discrete $J_C$, with discontinuity
set exactly $J_C$. Under the surreal normal-form embedding it is a subring
of the omnific integers.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For $f\in C$, the principal part
$p=T_{\leq0}f-f_0$ belongs to $B$ by \eqref{pma:ser:eq:trunccore}. Thus the formula
\[
 \lfloor f\rfloor_{J_C}
 =p+\lfloor f_0\rfloor
  -\mathbf1_{\{f_0\in\Z,\ f-T_{\leq0}f<0\}}
\]
defines an element of $C$ with no positive exponents and integer constant.
The same leading-term comparisons as in Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:commonip} prove
the integer-part inequalities and discrete order. Ring closure follows from
convolution: negative exponents cannot contribute to the constant term.
Every $j\in J_C$ equals $T_{\leq0}j$, so lies in the countable set $B$.
Closed discreteness also follows from Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:integerparts}.

For each nonnegative integer $n$, the map $T_{\leq n}:C\to B$, with $B$
given the discrete topology, is locally constant. The maps
$f\mapsto\lfloor T_{\leq n}f\rfloor_{J_C}$ are therefore continuous and
are eventually equal to the floor at each input. Only the first nonzero
positive term is needed to decide the possible correction. The interval
fibers and the sequence $a-t^n\to a$ give the exact discontinuity set as
before. Finally, replacing $t^q$ by $\omega^{-q}$ turns the nonpositive
support and integer constant conditions into the omnific normal-form
conditions.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The target topology matters]
The Baire class one assertion is about a \emph{discrete} integer-part target,
not just its inclusion in the field. It is stronger than merely producing
continuous real-valued approximants to the ordinary floor on $\R$.
Indeed, the ordinary floor $\R\to\Z_{\mathrm{discrete}}$ is not Baire class
one: every continuous map from connected $\R$ to discrete $\Z$ is constant,
and a pointwise convergent sequence of constant maps has a constant limit.
The locally constant prefix maps are a genuine non-Archimedean resource.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The common integer part satisfies open induction}

The classical relationship between integer parts of real closed fields and
open induction is often called the integer-part characterization of open
induction. For our specific ring the needed implication has a short proof.

\begin{proposition}[Open induction]\label{pma:ser:prop:iopen}
The nonnegative cone $\mathcal I_{k,\geq0}$ satisfies $Q+\mathrm{IOpen}$ in the
language of ordered arithmetic, with parameters allowed in the induction
scheme.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The elementary axioms of discretely ordered semiring arithmetic hold in the
nonnegative cone of any discretely ordered unital ring. Every positive
$a\in\Ik$ has predecessor $a-1\geq0$.

A quantifier-free formula in one variable with parameters from $\Ik$ is a
Boolean combination of polynomial equations and inequalities. In the real
closed field $\Lk$, the real roots of its finitely many nonzero polynomials
partition the line into finitely many intervals and points on which the
formula has constant truth value. Every nonempty trace of one of these pieces
on $\mathcal I_{k,\geq0}$ has a least element: the lower endpoint, when included and
already in $\Ik$, or the first integer-part element above that endpoint,
with $0$ serving as a lower bound when needed. The floor property supplies
this first element. Therefore every nonempty quantifier-free definable subset
of $\mathcal I_{k,\geq0}$ has a least element.

If a quantifier-free formula held at $0$ and were preserved by successor but
failed somewhere, its counterexamples would have a least element $a$.
Then $a>0$ and the formula holds at $a-1$, contradicting preservation by
successor. This is open induction.
\end{proof}

\noindent\writetag{} This is the route of \replabel{dsn:cor:openinduction} and
of source~03's \cref{pma:bor:thm:arithmetic}, which proves open induction for
$\A$; the Shepherdson model is classical \cite{Shepherdson}.

\begin{corollary}[Arbitrary countable support complexity in countable arithmetic]
For every countable ordinal $\alpha$, there is a real closed Polish
truncation-closed field $C\subseteq\ka((t^\Q))$ whose countable canonical
integer part $J_C$ contains an element with support order type $\alpha$.
The nonnegative cone of $J_C$ satisfies $Q+\mathrm{IOpen}$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Embed $\alpha$ into $\Q\cap(-2,-1)$ and take the Hahn series with coefficient
$1$ on that support. Put it in a strengthened hull using
Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:rchull}. Its support is negative and its constant is $0$,
so it belongs to $J_C$. The proof of Proposition~\ref{pma:ser:prop:iopen} applies
verbatim with this integer part and its real closed ambient field.
\end{proof}

There is no conflict with Glazer's result: the arithmetic model
$\mathcal I_{k,\geq0}$ is countable. Its uncountable real closed ambient field
$\Lk$ is not a discretely ordered arithmetic model. Moreover, $\Pk$ and
$\Lk$ share this same integer part even though only the latter supports
admissible Polish field arithmetic.

\subsection{Why the finite integer part does not extend to the full Hahn field}

The element
\[
 z=t^{-2}y=\sum_{n\geq2}t^{-1-1/n}\in\Hk,
\]
where $y$ is from \eqref{pma:ser:eq:boundedhahn}, has infinitely many negative terms.
For every $a\in\Ik$, at least one negative coefficient of $z-a$ remains
nonzero; its leading exponent is negative, so $|z-a|>1$. Hence $\Ik$ is
not an integer part of $\Hk$.

Replacing finite negative support in \eqref{pma:ser:eq:integerpart} by arbitrary
well-ordered negative support gives an integer part of $\Hk$, with the
same coefficientwise floor rule. This larger ring is uncountable: binary
choices along the support of $z$ already produce continuum many elements.
Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:integerparts} supplies another obstruction to admissible
Polish arithmetic on this full Hahn field. Here that argument agrees with,
but is less general than, Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:hahn}.

\section{Surreal realization and a formalization interface}
\label{pma:ser:sec:surreal}

\subsection{A set-sized embedding, not a topology on a proper class}

With $k=\ka\subseteq\R$, the normal-form assignment
\begin{equation}\label{pma:ser:eq:surreal}
 \iota\!\left(\sum_{q\in\Q}a_qt^q\right)
       =\sum_{q\in\Q}a_q\omega^{-q}
\end{equation}
realizes the three fields as set-sized subfields of the surreal numbers.
Well-ordered support in the $q$-variable becomes reverse-well-ordered support
in the Conway growth exponents $-q$. The normal-form rules preserve addition,
multiplication, and order; $t$ corresponds to $\omega^{-1}$.
The image of $\Ik$ has only positive growth exponents and an integer
constant term, so lies in $\Oz$.

This is a formal normal-form embedding. It does not assert that an arbitrary
infinite series can be evaluated at an infinite real or surreal argument by
ordinary analytic convergence. In particular, the direction of the exponent
change in \eqref{pma:ser:eq:surreal} is essential.
The full proper class $\No$ is never asserted to be a Polish space.
The series carriers have cardinality continuum and can be constructed as
subsets of the set $k^\Q$. Only afterwards are they identified with surreal
normal forms.

The project documentation is an interface to these structures, not a proof
that the new topological statements have already been formalized. The
reported kernel checks in ProveIt concern the repository's declared
constructions and theorems. No new kernel run was performed for this article.

\subsection{A modular Lean development plan}

A formalization need not begin with surreal arithmetic. Most of the proof is
independent of it. A suitable division into modules is as follows.

\paragraph{Category and ordered groups.}
Formalize the Baire-order local-bound lemma over a linearly ordered abelian
group, separately from the field specialization. Its interface should return
an order bound $a>0$ and a neighborhood inclusion, not an implicit assertion
that the ambient topology is the order topology. Import a proved Pettis
lemma and Borel-homomorphism automatic continuity at clearly stated instances.

\paragraph{Ordered rings and fields.}
Prove automatic discreteness using one scalar $a+1$, and prove the field
refinement theorem using $(a+1)/\varepsilon$. These are different lemmas:
quotient scalars are not available in a general ordered ring.
Then prove closed discreteness and countability of any integer-part subring
without assuming its carrier is measurable.

\paragraph{Series and topology.}
Construct left-finite coefficient functions as a subtype of $\Q\to k$.
Give finite-below-cutoff witnesses explicitly. Prove convolution finiteness,
the inverse construction, valuation identities, and metric completeness.
The countable dense subspace consists of finitely supported functions.
A separate theorem identifies the metric and order topologies.

\paragraph{Obstruction certificates.}
Formalize the binary family from Lemma~\ref{pma:ser:lem:separated}; the only infinite
support needed for the negative result has order type at most $\omega$.
For Puiseux, package the general countable proper-subfield-cover theorem,
then prove each lattice-support stage is closed. This separation prevents a
proof of incompleteness of one metric from being mistaken for a proof of
absolute non-Polishability.

\paragraph{Integer parts and measurable floors.}
First prove \eqref{pma:ser:eq:floor} in the left-finite series subtype. Then identify
this ring with the corresponding set-sized subring of the repository's
omnific integers. The exact discontinuity theorem and the pointwise eventually
constant sequence $(F_n)$ can be formalized independently of the full surreal
field.

\paragraph{Descriptive complexity.}
The explicit maps in \eqref{pma:ser:eq:rowcode} and the $\FIN$ reduction are the
finite combinatorial portion. The well-founded-tree reduction and
Lusin--Souslin step belong in a separate descriptive-set-theory layer.
They are not required for the core Hahn classification or the positive
Levi-Civita construction.

This architecture leaves no proper-class quantification in the main proof.
Universe parameters are needed only when identifying the set-sized series
carrier with a universe-relative surreal implementation. No additional large
cardinal assumption is used.

\section{Consequences for exact symbolic arithmetic}
\label{pma:ser:sec:computing}

An exact coefficient stream and a valuation-Cauchy name provide different
information. A coefficient stream reports one coefficient at a time. A
valuation-Cauchy name eventually fixes \emph{all} coefficients below a
specified cutoff. In a left-finite representation, certifying a completed
prefix therefore includes a support bound, not just a list of currently
observed nonzero terms.

Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:lc} supports continuous arithmetic in the second
representation. Proposition~\ref{pma:ser:prop:sameborel} says that the measurable
information is the same, but it does not turn these representations into the
same effective oracle. No computability claim follows merely from Borel
equivalence.

For an exact rational-coefficient prototype one can represent finite
truncations by dictionaries $q\mapsto a_q$ with $q,a_q\in\Q$.
When multiplying to cutoff $B$, lower bounds on the two supports determine
which input prefixes suffice: terms of the first factor above $B-v(g)$,
and terms of the second above $B-v(f)$, cannot affect the result. For
inversion of $a t^q(1+h)$, a positive lower bound
$\delta\leq v(h)$ bounds the number of geometric powers needed below any
fixed cutoff. These are mathematically certified finite reductions, not
numerical approximations to real coefficients.

Floor is different. Its Baire class one approximants may change once the
first nonzero infinitesimal term is revealed at an integer boundary. No
continuous exact floor algorithm on the whole field can exist, because the
mathematical function is discontinuous there. The theorem pinpoints the
necessary limitation instead of attributing it to a poor choice of code.

The accompanying Python program uses rational exponents and coefficients to
check finite algebraic identities, truncated geometric inverses, the floor
inequalities, and finite instances of the support witnesses. The deterministic run passed 20,116 finite assertions; its output
records the counts by test family. These tests catch implementation mistakes in
the supplied examples; they cannot establish Baire category, quantification
over all Polish topologies, infinite support completeness, or real closedness.
The conventional proofs are the basis of those assertions.

\section{Further research questions and concrete targets}
\label{pma:ser:sec:questions}

The following questions are proposed next steps. They are not asserted to be
previously published open problems, and no novelty claim is inferred from
failure to locate an answer in the inspected sources.

\begin{question}[Intermediate-field rigidity]\label{pma:ser:q:intermediate}
Let $k$ be countable and ordered, and let
$\Pk\subseteq F\subseteq k((t^\Q))$.
If $F$ has an admissible Polish topology, must its valuation topology be
complete, and must the admissible topology equal the valuation topology?
\end{question}
The prefix criterion gives countably many prefixes, but it does not prove
completeness of a coarser topology. A proof must close precisely that gap;
one cannot appeal to the open mapping theorem until both topologies are known
to be Polish.

\begin{question}[Classification of Polish hulls]
Within a fixed standard Borel family of coefficient-observable parameters,
what is the descriptive complexity of equality and inclusion between the
countable-parameter hulls $F_A$? Can one construct useful canonical dense
cores or invariants for their ordered-field isomorphism types?
\end{question}
The existence of proper Polish extensions of $\Lk$ and the absence of maximal
ones are settled by Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:hulls} and Corollary~\ref{pma:ser:cor:nomax}.
The remaining issue is classification, not existence. The ambient parameter
space must be specified: the full Hahn coefficient domain is not standard
Borel.

\begin{question}[Minimal scalar hypotheses]
How few Borel scalar maps suffice to force the order topology in the
rational-exponent setting? Can a specified countable set of monomial
multiplications replace all scalar maps under additional algebraic or
truncation-closure assumptions?
\end{question}
The current proof uses a scalar depending on the local order bound and the
target interval. An answer should identify which part of that freedom is
essential, rather than dropping scalar regularity altogether.

\begin{question}[Order-free non-real-closed fields]
For countable ordered $k$ that is not real closed, can the Hahn and Puiseux
nonexistence statements be derived from a Polish additive group and jointly
Borel multiplication alone, without requiring the displayed order to have the
Baire property?
\end{question}
Corollary~\ref{pma:ser:cor:realorder} settles the real closed case. In general a field
may have several orders, and the image-of-squares argument does not identify
one positive cone.

\begin{question}[Other support completions]
Extend the left-finite construction and its classification to countable
ordered exponent groups of higher rank. Which support restrictions give
separable complete field topologies, and which admit a countable cofinal
valuation scale but still fail by a proper-subfield cover?
\end{question}
The inverse proof in Proposition~\ref{pma:ser:prop:lcfield} explicitly uses
$n\delta\to+\infty$ in an Archimedean exponent group. It cannot be copied
unchanged into a lexicographic higher-rank group.

\begin{question}[Descriptive ranks beyond the trichotomy]
Which Borel ranks can occur for coefficient-code domains of admissibly Polish
subfields of $k((t^\Q))$? Is there a useful algebraic characterization of
when a Borel support family yields a Polishable additive group?
\end{question}
Table~\ref{pma:ser:tab:trichotomy} shows that a smaller rank is not sufficient.
A productive refinement should combine support closure, completeness of
prefixes, and the proper-subfield obstruction.

\begin{question}[The least support bound of a one-parameter hull]
For $y=\sum_{n\geq2}t^{1-1/n}$, determine the least countable ordinal bounding
all support order types in $F_{\{y\}}$. How does that least bound change when
real closure and closure under all rational truncations are imposed before
metric completion?
\end{question}
Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:rankbound} proves existence of a bound but does not compute
it. A solution would require quantitative control of support order types
under convolution, inversion, algebraic extension, and stabilized-prefix
limits. It would turn the qualitative local-hull theorem into an ordinal
complexity calculation.

\begin{question}[Other integer parts and floor discontinuities]
Classify integer parts of $\Lk$ and the larger real closed Polish hulls up to
field automorphisms. Which floors admit canonical or effective locally
constant approximants for presentations not built from truncations?
\end{question}
Countability and closed discreteness hold for every integer part by
Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:integerparts}. The discontinuity set of its floor is also
its integer part in the order topology. The strengthened hulls retain explicit truncation approximants. The
remaining constructive issue is canonical or effective approximation for
other presentations, together with a classification of the integer parts.

\begin{question}[A uniform effective presentation]
For computably presented $k$, construct a certified Type-2 representation of
$\Lk$ with computable arithmetic, explicit precision transfer for products
and inverses, and a finite-mind-change floor procedure for $k=\ka$.
\end{question}
The representation must include effective support-completion information.
Bare enumerations of nonzero coefficients do not suffice to know that a
prefix is complete. Any claimed effective embedding into Baire space should
state which oracle representation is being used.

\begin{question}[Formal proof milestones]
Can the local order-bound theorem, automatic discreteness, and full Hahn
classification be formalized as a small reusable library independent of the
full surreal development? What is the minimal imported descriptive-set-theory
interface for the exact code-complexity theorems?
\end{question}
These are concrete implementation tasks. The first milestone is the
category-to-order bridge; the coanalytic lower bound should not block the
rest of the project.

\begin{question}[Positive cones versus groups of differences]\label{pma:ser:q:cones}
Characterize Borel discretely ordered semirings whose positive cones admit
Polish continuous addition but whose groups of differences are not Polishable
with measurable multiplication. Which hypotheses force a topology to extend
from the cone to its group completion?
\end{question}
This returns directly to the boundary emphasized by Glazer's arithmetic
questions and the earlier Presburger constructions. Theorem~\ref{pma:ser:thm:discrete}
provides an obstruction on the group side, not a classification of monoid
presentations.

\noindent\writetag{} This is source~08's \cref{pma:lc:q:completion}; source~06's
cone of $\Z+t\R[t]$ is a data point (\cref{pma:cp:sec:meet}).

\begin{question}[Weak choice and category]
Which portions of the classification survive in weaker choice settings?
Separate the dependent choices used to build the bounded increasing exponent
sequence, the Baire theorem, and the selections of representatives of
countably many prefix classes.
\end{question}
The present paper works in ZFC and makes no choice-free assertion. A
foundational analysis would be a distinct project rather than an automatic
consequence of the set-sized constructions.

\section{Conclusions of Part V}

The main dividing line is not whether exact infinite series can be assigned
Borel codes. It is whether their arithmetic, order, and completeness fit
within a separable Polish group.
A Baire-measurable order and Borel scalar arithmetic force local order bounds
and hence the order topology. Full Hahn support then admits too many
mutually separated prefixes, except in the countable-coefficient Laurent
case. Puiseux support avoids that obstruction but fails a different category
test because it is a countable union of proper measurable subfields.
Left-finite completion avoids both obstructions and supplies a unique Polish
topological field. Countable-parameter hulls go further: arbitrary countable
Hahn data fit into a Polish subfield, even with real closedness and truncation
closure over real algebraic coefficients. There is no maximal Polish
intermediate field, while the exact cover number of the full field is the
continuum.

The integer-part consequences connect this topological classification to
arithmetic and surreal normal forms, including arbitrary prescribed countable
support types inside suitable countable integer parts. A Polish ordered field cannot hide an
uncountable discrete integer part, even a nonmeasurable one. The real
algebraic-coefficient Levi-Civita field nevertheless supports a countable
omnific integer part with open induction, and its floor has exactly
controlled discontinuities. These results supply a precise next layer beyond
Borel support analysis while leaving topology rigidity on intermediate fields and the broader
positive-cone classification open.

\appendix

\section{Worked examples and an audit of the hypotheses (source 05)}
\label{pma:pr:sec:audit}

\subsection{A negative constant term is compatible with positivity}

In $\MR$, take
\[
 x=(\tfrac32,-17),\qquad y=(\tfrac14,6).
\]
Both are positive, and
\[
 x+y=(\tfrac74,-11).
\]
Division of $x$ by $5$ gives
\[
 q_5(x)=(\tfrac3{10},-4),\qquad r_5(x)=3,
\]
because $-17=5(-4)+3$. The quotient is positive because its real
coordinate is positive. A definition that incorrectly demanded a
nonnegative integer coordinate for every element would break the
predecessor and division axioms.

\subsection{A nonstandard finite-candidate calculation}

Let $L=(\tfrac12,-5)$, $U=(\tfrac23,0)$, and require
\[
 y\equiv(\tfrac7{13},2)\pmod{3G}.
\]
The real coefficient of a congruence parameter is irrelevant because
$\R$ is divisible. Among $L,L+1,L+2$, the candidate $L+1$ has integer
coordinate $-4\equiv2\pmod3$, so it is a witness. Every candidate lies
below $U$, since its leading coefficient is $1/2<2/3$.
No iteration a nonstandard number of times is used: the finite search is
justified by full invariance on $3G$-cosets.

\subsection{A Baire convergence that order cannot detect continuously}

Let $e_j$ be the rational unit vector at coordinate $j$. Then
\[
 (e_j,0)\to(0,0),\qquad (e_j,0)>n\one
 \quad\text{for every fixed standard }n.
\]
The initial coefficients eventually agree with zero to every prescribed
finite length. In omnific notation this sequence is
\[
 \omega,\quad\omega^{1/2},\quad\omega^{1/3},\quad\ldots,
\]
which converges to zero in the \emph{specified coefficient topology},
not in the arithmetic order topology. This is a concrete illustration,
not a claim about an unspecified topology on surreal numbers.

\subsection{Hypothesis checklist}

\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{.30\textwidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{.62\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Potential pitfall & Resolution in the proofs\\
\midrule
\endhead
Only the division axioms are checked &
\Cref{pma:pr:thm:qe,pma:pr:cor:fullpresburger,pma:pr:prop:least} establish the complete theory
and every additive induction instance.\\[.6em]
An open ray is given an incomplete metric &
\Cref{pma:pr:sec:topology} provides a compatible logarithmic metric on each
open ray and a complete metric on the whole sum.\\[.6em]
The intended topology is silently replaced by the order topology &
Both topologies are explicitly distinguished; the arithmetic order
topology is discrete.\\[.6em]
Standard-shift invariance is used with a nonstandard quotient &
\Cref{pma:pr:ex:falseperiod} exhibits the failure; \cref{pma:pr:lem:finite} uses
invariance on $mG$-cosets instead.\\[.6em]
The all-zero rational branch is lost &
The nonnegative-cone tree in \eqref{pma:pr:eq:zerobranch} explicitly includes it.\\[.6em]
Computing an output tag secretly requires deciding equality of streams &
For a negative output integer coordinate, a negative input supplies a
finite bound on the first positive coefficient.\\[.6em]
A type-theoretic ``computable model'' is confused with a countable one &
The presentation is Type-2 on all Baire sequences; not every element is
computable, and order has only a limit decision procedure.\\[.6em]
The omnific image is assumed closed under multiplication &
It is only an additive fragment. \Cref{pma:pr:thm:no_semiring} rules out even
an alternative semiring multiplication on the same carrier.\\[.6em]
A proper class is treated as a Polish space &
Only explicit set-sized images and a sufficiently large set-sized
birthday cutoff are used.\\[.6em]
Existing repository proofs are credited with new theorems &
The formalization roadmap distinguishes the inspected integer theorems
from the additional nonstandard and topological obligations.\\[.6em]
Numerical testing is mistaken for a proof &
The exact test scope is documented in \cref{pma:pr:sec:formalization}; no formal
verification claim is made for this article.\\[.6em]
An affirmative proof is mistaken for certified historical priority &
The source question and version are identified; independent priority and
refereeing remain separate from the mathematical proof.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\section{Reproduction and the scope of the executable checks (source 04)}\label{pma:pr:app:reproduction}

The package requires a standard LaTeX installation for the article and
Python 3.10 or later for the companion code. The Python program uses only
the standard library and exact \code{fractions.Fraction} arithmetic.
From the package directory, run
\begin{lstlisting}
python3 verification.py --output verification_results.json
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error polish_presburger.tex
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error polish_presburger.tex
\end{lstlisting}
A \code{Makefile} supplies equivalent targets. The delivered run used seed
$20261003$ and passed $75{,}079$ assertions. Its breakdown is:

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lr@{}}
\toprule
Check category & Assertions\\
\midrule
Rational, integer, and pairing codes & $6{,}468$\\
Finite group and order identities & $9{,}955$\\
Exact quotient and remainder & $2{,}151$\\
Boundary witness transfer & $21{,}037$\\
Finite candidate comparison & $1{,}200$\\
External periodicity counterexample & $2{,}321$\\
Positive-pivot matrix tests & $18{,}000$\\
Monus integer-coordinate obstruction & $400$\\
Baire-code round trips & $97$\\
Baire quotient and remainder prefixes & $9{,}700$\\
Baire addition prefixes & $3{,}750$\\
\midrule
Total & $75{,}079$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The finite group tests use rational coefficient vectors and signed integer
coordinates. The boundary tests examine witnesses from a specified finite
pool and compare them with the exact candidate set. They do not exhaust
an infinite group. The matrix tests use finite initial pieces of matrices
satisfying the positive-pivot conditions. The stream tests check finite
prefixes of deterministic infinite oracles, including long zero runs.
The monus tests verify exact coordinate obstructions along finite portions
of the sequences used in the proof, not topological convergence by numerical
approximation.

The model-theoretic, infinite-matrix, and topological statements rest on the
proofs in the manuscript. Tests are useful for detecting sign errors,
incorrect negative quotients, wrong coordinate offsets, and mismatched
coding branches, but a successful run is not a substitute for those proofs.

this report its program is \fn{code/04-polish-presburger-verification.py}, its
recorded run \fn{data/04-polish-presburger-verification_results.json}, its
Makefile \fn{code/04-polish-presburger-Makefile} (which still names
\fn{polish_presburger.tex}, \fn{verification.py} and
\fn{verification_results.json}), and its manuscript is merged into this
\fn{article.tex}. The program's default output is
\fn{verification_results.json} in the working directory, so it is rerun with
an explicit output path outside the report (README). The intake reran it on a
copy with Python~3.14.4: 75{,}079 assertions passed, and the record was
identical up to line endings.

\section{A compact proof-dependency map (source 04)}\label{pma:pr:app:dependency}

The answer can be read independently of the optional extensions:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \text{discrete split group and explicit division}\\
 \Downarrow\\
 \text{finite boundary criterion and quantifier elimination}\\
 \Downarrow\\
 M_D\models\Th(\N;0,1,+)\\
 \Downarrow\quad\text{together with the chosen topology}\\
 \text{Polishness, continuous addition, and fixed division}.
 \end{gathered}
\]
For the real example, \cref{pma:pr:sec:real} completes the argument at that point.
For literal Baire space, add only the two chart codings in
\cref{pma:pr:s04:sec:baire}. The computation of the monus locus, the embedding
classification, the growth-order obstruction, and the surreal realization
are subsequent results; none is a hidden prerequisite for the affirmative
construction.

\section{Source audit, contribution ledger, and reproducibility (source 03)}
\label{pma:bor:app:audit}

\subsection{Repository material inspected}

The repository branch was resolved to the commit printed in
Section~\ref{pma:bor:sec:intro}. The following guides were inspected in selected ranges at that revision:
\begin{quote}\small\ttfamily
Algebra/SurrealNumbers/README.md\par
Algebra/SurrealNumbers/docs/README.md\par
Algebra/SurrealNumbers/docs/\par
\quad foundations-and-computation/omnific-notations/README.md
\end{quote}
The first guide supplies the context of the omnific ring, its least positive
element, and the floor boundary correction. The notations guide reports the
existing Kleene--Brouwer support-validity classification and carefully states
the limited scope of its finite checks. The present article does not certify
all statements in either guide.

The external mathematical inputs are Newton--Puiseux, the classical Hahn-field
construction, Hausdorff's characterization of countable scattered orders,
standard descriptive-set-theoretic facts, and Glazer's Corollary 1.
Analytic boundedness and the needed Kleene--Brouwer reduction are proved in
the text. Shepherdson's theorem is contextualized and its needed direction
is also proved directly.

\subsection{Contribution ledger}

\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.29\textwidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.65\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Statement & Status in this report\\
\midrule
\endhead
General coanalytic support hardness & Prior classical mechanism; already
recorded for omnific notations in ProveIt. Reproved to make the reductions explicit.\\
Scattered/support dichotomy & Proved from Hausdorff theory and the fixed tree
reduction; global priority not asserted.\\
Observable full-Hahn criterion & Proved, including Borel field operations on
the scattered side and exclusion of even repeated observable names on the
nonscattered side.\\
Exact $\Z^d$ Borel levels & Proved with explicit continuous reductions and a
separate sharp support-order bound.\\
Uniform observable support bound & Classical analytic boundedness specialized
to coefficient families; a proof is supplied.\\
Fixed-interval binary localization & Explicit application of the support
reduction, not advertised as a new foundational lower-bound method.\\
Principal-part integer part and floor & Shepherdson-type classical model;
negative-infinitesimal correction also already in ProveIt.\\
Exact cancellation loci & Proved for the stated Polish finite-normal-form
topology, including closed-piece continuity and zero-factor cases.\\
No compatible continuous addition & Direct application of Glazer after all
model and Borel hypotheses have been verified.\\
Formal verification & No Lean proof delivered. Python checks only finite
exact-rational identities, order tests, floor cases, and finite embedding data.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\subsection{Finite verification artifact}

The archive contains \texttt{verify.py}, a Python 3.10+ standard-library-only
program, and the actual result \texttt{verification.json}. The recorded run
used Python 3.13.5, deterministic seed \texttt{2601003}, and passed
\textbf{84,388 assertions}. These include 6,000 ring-law checks, 2,000 closure
checks, 2,000 positive-cone checks, 999 discreteness checks, 7,500 floor-interval
checks, 63 negative-infinitesimal boundary checks, 25 cancellation checks,
65,792 rational Kleene--Brouwer embedding checks on 256 nodes, and 9 coherence
and branch-approximation checks.

The finite embedding is an initial segment of one fixed midpoint construction:
nodes are enumerated by the weight $\sum_i(s_i+1)$, and each new node is
inserted between its previously assigned neighbors. This checks the direction
of the order reversal and the rational band. It does not test the
well-foundedness of arbitrary infinite trees.

To reproduce:
\begin{verbatim}
python verify.py --output verification.json
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error article.tex
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error article.tex
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error article.tex
\end{verbatim}
The bibliography is internal, so no BibTeX run or network access is required.
The finite checks are useful for catching sign, support, and boundary mistakes;
they neither prove descriptive completeness nor replace the mathematical
proofs or an independent review.

\noindent\writetag{} In this report source~03's program is
\fn{code/03-borel-presentations-verify.py}, its recorded run
\fn{data/03-borel-presentations-verification.json}, and its provenance record
\fn{data/03-borel-presentations-provenance.json}; the record still describes
03's own 23-page PDF, which is not shipped. Its manuscript is merged into this
\fn{article.tex}. The intake reran the program on a copy with Python~3.14.4:
84{,}388 assertions passed, and the record differed from the delivered one only
in the interpreter version and line endings.

\section{Limitations and non-claims of the sources}\label{pma:app:nonclaims}

Every limitation stated by a source is kept. They are grouped by source; the
items written for the merge come last.

\paragraph{Source 05.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=05.\arabic*]
\item The construction answers Glazer's Question~2 \emph{as written};
independent bibliographic priority has not been established. The literature
search verified the question in the source PDF and checked the arXiv record;
it did not establish the absence of an earlier solution.
\item Classical Presburger elimination, the $\Z$-group facts and elementary
topological-group facts are not claimed as new; the lexicographic algebra is
classical.
\item The manuscript is AI-assisted, not independently refereed and not
Lean-verified; no new Lean or Rocq formalization is included; the proposed
Lean modules are a plan, not existing files.
\item The executable tests are finite instances and stream prefixes; they do
not quantify over arbitrary elements of $\Q^{\N}$, and finite prefixes do not
decide equality of infinite streams.
\item No answer to Glazer's Question~1 is claimed; no uncountable Polish model
of Peano arithmetic; no continuous arithmetic order on these nondiscrete
spaces; no terminating decision of equality of arbitrary infinite sequences
(comparison is approximated with at most one change).
\item The omnific maps are additive and order-preserving, not ring
embeddings; the topologies are specified coefficient topologies, not a
canonical topology on $\Oz$; no topology on a proper class is used; all
constructions are set-sized in ZFC.
\item Neither model admits any unital semiring multiplication with the given
addition and unit; nothing contradicts Glazer's obstruction for stronger
arithmetic, and the no-semiring argument does not replace it.
\item The repository's integer Cooper lemmas are correct on \texttt{Int}; the
existence of the integer eliminator does not certify the theorem for a new
carrier; the original integer theorem need not be weakened or retracted.
\item The questions are proposed directions, not certified as open in the
literature; the user's repository was not modified.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 04.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=04.\arabic*]
\item The construction answers Question~2 as printed; historical priority has
not been established; the literature check did not establish that the
construction has never appeared elsewhere.
\item The manuscript is unrefereed; no new theorem is represented as
machine-checked; Lean/Rocq certification is not supplied.
\item The classical $\Z$-group characterization and quantifier elimination
are not claimed as new.
\item The difference between one-step periodicity and residue invariance is a
porting requirement, not a reported bug in the integer proof.
\item Baire coordinates encode the arithmetic structure and are not base
digits; the stream API cannot decide totality of an arbitrary oracle, and
partial, negative or noninteger entries are not supported.
\item The source audit was limited to the named files at its pin; no
repository files were modified and no repository-wide build was performed;
the formalization stages are proposals, not assertions about existing
declarations.
\item The tests are finite, exact and partial; the model-theoretic,
infinite-matrix and topological statements rest on the proofs.
\item No third-party font files or paper copies are included.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 03.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=03.\arabic*]
\item A research draft, not refereed and not a Lean proof; global priority
and independent correctness review are not established.
\item The repository's coanalytic support obstruction and the
negative-infinitesimal floor correction are credited, not claimed as new; the
integer-part model is of Shepherdson type, not new; analytic boundedness and
the Hausdorff characterization are classical.
\item The full-Hahn nonpresentation results need observable coefficients and
coverage of the whole field; they do not exclude unobservable Borel
presentations or Borel real closed subfields.
\item Only addition is shown impossible to make continuous; Glazer's
multiplication obstruction (his Theorem~2) is not transferred, since only open
induction is proved for $\PP$; continuous multiplication under another
compatible topology is open.
\item The zero loci are not claimed minimal in cardinality, dimension or
descriptive rank; countable closed-piece continuity is not identified with the
Baire-one class.
\item The dichotomy is per fixed $L$, not about the class of scattered orders,
nor uniform in a code of $L$; the Hausdorff decomposition used need not be
computable; the uniform support bound need not be computable from a code.
\item No general computability over arbitrary real coefficients is claimed.
\item All results are set-sized in ZFC; no class-sized Polish topology, global
choice or large cardinal is used.
\item The Python checks are finite (they do not test well-foundedness of
infinite trees, completeness, or the external theorems); the formalization
plan is proposed only; no repository files were changed and the whole
repository was not audited.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 07.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=07.\arabic*]
\item The construction answers Question~2 as printed; the claim that it is the
first published resolution is not made. The literature search was targeted,
not exhaustive; the precise place in the literature of the cone theorem, the
Baire family and the rank computations is left to specialist review.
\item The lexicographic construction of a $\Z$-group is classical; classical
model theory and standard descriptive set theory are inputs; no historical
priority for the formulation of \cref{pma:cp:thm:cones} is needed or claimed,
measurable orders on locally compact groups having been studied by Asmar and
Montgomery-Smith.
\item No new result has been checked by Lean or Rocq; no Lean or Rocq build was
run; the repository was used read-only; no new formal theorem is claimed; no
publication priority or external peer review is claimed.
\item Only first-order induction is claimed, not second-order induction over
external subsets.
\item No computable homeomorphism is claimed for an arbitrary presentation of
a countable ordinal; no classification of the pure underlying orders is
claimed; the oscillation-rank convention is fixed and not identified with
every convention for difference-hierarchy ranks.
\item The omnific images are not claimed initial, closed under surreal
multiplication or under surreal-analytic operations; the transported
topology is not the surreal order topology; every model is set-sized and the
class $\No$ is used only as a definable ambient class.
\item Glazer's Question~1 is not answered, and the metatheoretic strength of
his theorem with multiplication is not determined.
\item The LinkedIn page supplied with the brief could not be read; the
repository search covered \texttt{.tex}, \texttt{.md} and \texttt{.lean} files
at the pin, not every branch or binary artifact.
\item The questions are proposed directions, not asserted to be historically
new or unresolved in all of the literature.
\item No finite numerical experiment is offered as evidence; compilation and
visual inspection verify the document, not the mathematics; the proof checks
made during preparation are not external refereeing or proof-assistant
certification.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Source 06.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=06.\arabic*]
\item No machine-checked certification and no unrestricted
publication-priority claim is made; the literature search is a bounded
bibliographic finding, not a proof of universal priority.
\item The lexicographic $\Z$-group construction and its model theory are
classical (Je\v r\'abek); rational polynomial Presburger examples appear in
lecture notes (Cook--Pitassi); the polynomial ring has classical precedents
and occurs in the repository.
\item The computability claims are Type-2 claims about streams: no computable
enumeration of the uncountable model and no decidability of equality of
streams are asserted, so there is no conflict with Tennenbaum's theorem; the
continuum family is not claimed computable.
\item The ordinal scales are classes of magnitude, not $\omega_1$-sequences in
one order; there is no conflict with the obstruction of Enayat, Hamkins and
Wcis\l o.
\item The failure of open induction concerns $\Z+t\R[t]$ only and does not
transfer to $\Oz$; the analogous topology on the signed ring is not a ring
topology, and partial subtraction on the cone is not continuous.
\item The isolated-zero theorem does not imply that every standard numeral is
isolated; it uses no induction inside the model, no standard division,
exponentiation, saturation or integer-part theorem.
\item The surreal embeddings are additive; no closure under surreal
multiplication is asserted; the topologies are transported, not inherited
from a topology on the class $\No$.

\section{An explicit induction check after quantifier elimination (source 07)}
\label{pma:cp:app:induction}

For readers concerned that discrete order and division might conceal a
missing induction argument, the standard quantifier-elimination input
also yields a direct least-element proof.

Let $G$ be any $\Z$-group with positive cone $M$. After quantifier
elimination, a unary definable set with parameters is a finite Boolean
combination of linear inequalities and congruences. Division by fixed
standard coefficients changes each inequality into an upper or lower
bound on the variable. Taking a common standard modulus and partitioning
at finitely many endpoints writes the set as a finite union of cells
\[
I\cap\{x\in M:x\equiv r\pmod d\},
\]
where $I$ is an arithmetic interval, possibly with an infinite end.
Empty cells may be discarded.

Every nonempty cell has a least element. Intersect with $M$ and replace
a strict finite lower bound by its successor; if there is no effective
lower bound, use zero. This gives a closed lower endpoint $a\in M$.
Choose the unique standard $t\in\{0,\ldots,d-1\}$ such that
$a+t\equiv r\pmod d$. Then $a+t$ is the first element of that residue
class at or above $a$. If the cell is nonempty, $a+t$ also satisfies its
upper bound, since it precedes any other candidate in the cell. Thus it
is the cell's minimum. Taking the least of finitely many minima shows that
every nonempty unary definable subset of $M$ has a minimum.

Now let a definable $D\subseteq M$ contain zero and be closed under
successor. If its complement is nonempty, choose its least element $b$.
Then $b>0$ and its predecessor $b-1$ lies in $D$. Successor closure
puts $b$ in $D$, a contradiction. This proves every first-order
induction instance with parameters. The only imported step is classical
quantifier elimination, explicitly identified in
Section~\ref{pma:cp:sec:background}; no topological assumption enters.

\noindent\writetag{} This is a second route to the induction clause of
\cref{pma:pr:cor:fullpresburger}; source~06's \cref{pma:cp:app:qe} and
source~08's \cref{pma:lc:thm:qe} prove the elimination itself again.

\section{A self-contained Presburger elimination argument (source 06)}
\label{pma:cp:app:qe}

This appendix proves the algebraic characterization used in the main
constructions. It is included to make the induction claim fully
checkable without relying only on a reference to a characterization
theorem.

\subsection{Division in a \texorpdfstring{$\Z$}{Z}-group}

Let $G$ be a discretely ordered abelian group with least positive
element $1$ and divisible quotient $G/\Z$. Fix a positive standard
integer $n$. For each $g\in G$, divisibility of the quotient gives
$h\in G$ with $g-nh=k\cdot1$ for some standard integer $k$.
Write $k=nq+r$ in ordinary $\Z$, where $0\leq r<n$. Then
\[
 g=n(h+q\cdot1)+r\cdot1.
\]
This representation is unique: if two remainders $r,r'$ in the
specified finite interval differ by $n$ times a group element,
discreteness forces that element to be zero. Explicitly, a positive
difference would be at least $n$, and a negative one at most $-n$,
whereas $|r-r'|<n$.

Conversely, this division property makes $G/\Z$ divisible. Thus
we can work with the ordered-group axioms, discreteness, and this
schema of standard division algorithms.

\subsection{Eliminating one quantified variable}

Expand the language by predicates $D_n(u)$, for standard $n\geq1$,
with the interpretation $u\in nG$. Every group term is integer
linear. To eliminate an existential quantifier, first put its
quantifier-free matrix in disjunctive normal form and treat each
conjunction separately. Split disequalities into the two strict
inequalities and use discreteness to replace weak inequalities
by strict inequalities with a shift of $1$. Atoms not involving
the quantified variable are retained as parameter conditions.

Suppose the variable is $x$. Let $L$ be the least common multiple
of the nonzero absolute coefficients of $x$ in the conjunction,
or $1$ if there are none. Replace $Lx$ by $y$ and add $D_L(y)$.
For an inequality or equality involving $a x+u$, multiply by the
positive integer $L/|a|$; its coefficient of $y$ becomes $1$ or
$-1$. For a divisibility literal, use
\begin{equation}
 D_n(a x+u)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 D_{nm}(\operatorname{sign}(a)y+mu),
 \qquad m=L/|a|.
 \label{pma:cp:eq:div-scale}
\end{equation}
This is valid because, in a torsion-free group,
$v\in nG$ if and only if $mv\in nmG$. The same equivalence handles
negated divisibility. Thus every occurrence of $y$ has coefficient
$1$ or $-1$.

If an equality specifies $y$, substitute its value. Otherwise the
conjunction consists of finitely many lower bounds $a_i<y$, upper
bounds $y<b_j$, and positive or negative congruence conditions,
together with parameter-only literals.

Let $N$ be a common multiple of all congruence moduli, including
$L$, and split into the finitely many possibilities
$y\equiv r\pmod N$ for $0\leq r<N$. In each branch, a condition
$D_n(\pm y+u)$ is equivalent to the parameter condition
$D_n(\pm r+u)$. Only the one residue condition modulo $N$ and the
bounds now involve $y$.

If lower bounds are present, divide into finitely many cases
choosing their greatest value $a$; this is expressed by
parameter comparisons. Among
\[
 a+1,a+2,\ldots,a+N
\]
there is exactly one member of the chosen residue class. It is
the least member of that class above $a$. To justify minimality,
if $y>a$ has the same residue and lies below that candidate,
their positive difference is a multiple of $N$ smaller than
$N$, contradicting discreteness. Thus, when upper bounds are
also present, existence is equivalent to the finite disjunction
\[
 \bigvee_{k=1}^N
 \left(D_N(a+k-r)\ \wedge\ \bigwedge_j(a+k<b_j)\right).
\]
If there are no upper bounds, the candidate always works.
If there are upper bounds but no lower bounds, a residue-class
element exists among the $N$ positions just below the least
upper bound. If neither type of bound is present, the standard
representative $r$ itself works.

This eliminates one existential quantifier. Universal quantifiers
are handled by negation, and induction on formulas gives quantifier
elimination in the expanded language. The output consists of
Boolean combinations of integer-linear comparisons and standard
congruences.

\subsection{Completeness and induction in the nonnegative cone}

A closed quantifier-free formula in this language concerns only
standard numerals and their congruences and comparisons. Its truth
is the same in every $\Z$-group and in ordinary $\Z$.
Consequently every $\Z$-group is elementarily equivalent to $\Z$.

The structure $G_{\geq0}$ is interpreted in $G$ by restricting
quantifiers to $x\geq0$. Its addition, constants, and order are the
restrictions of those in $G$. Every sentence true in
$(\N,0,1,+,<)$ therefore holds in the cone.

In particular, for every additive formula $\varphi(x,\bar a)$ the
sentence
\[
 \forall\bar a\,
 \left[
 \left(\varphi(0,\bar a)\wedge
  \forall x\bigl(\varphi(x,\bar a)\to\varphi(x+1,\bar a)\bigr)\right)
 \to\forall x\,\varphi(x,\bar a)
 \right]
\]
holds. Parameters are covered because their universal closure is
part of the sentence. Multiplication is absent from this
language; the argument therefore makes no assertion about the
polynomial model's formula $x^2<t$.

\noindent\writetag{} This is a second route to \cref{pma:pr:thm:qe} and the
induction clause of \cref{pma:pr:cor:fullpresburger}, by the finite search of
\cref{pma:pr:lem:finite}.

\section{Provenance and verification record (source 06)}
\label{pma:cp:app:verification}

\paragraph{Mathematical dependencies.}
The main constructions use ordered-group algebra, elementary
Presburger elimination, standard facts about Polish spaces, and
explicit coordinate arguments. The isolated-zero theorem additionally
uses analytic/coanalytic separation and fair product measure.
Its tail zero-one step is proved in the text. The surreal realizations
are explanatory embeddings and are not premises of any of the main
topological theorems.

\paragraph{Independent scrutiny.}
The real and Baire models, the polynomial topology, the distinction
between additive and open induction, and the isolated-zero argument
were subjected to separate proof reviews during preparation. Those
reviews specifically checked complete metrizability rather than mere
separability, negative integer slices, the parameter version of
induction, joint continuity, cancellation, and the tail-event
invariance. The final proof omits an unnecessary Cantor-injectivity
claim and never assumes continuous subtraction. These reviews are
mathematical checks, not formal proof-assistant certification or
external peer review.

\paragraph{Repository audit.}
The repository comparison used its path inventory and selected
relevant files at the pinned commit. No full-repository theorem
audit or Lean rebuild was performed. Existing exploratory reports
are credited for their stated algebraic content, with the main
results here proved independently.

\paragraph{Reproducibility.}
This source is self-contained and uses standard \LaTeX{} packages.
It can be compiled with
\texttt{latexmk -pdf polish\_presburger.tex}.
No external image, private data, bibliography processor, or downloaded
repository is needed to reproduce the PDF. The accompanying ZIP
contains this source, its compiled PDF, and a short build-and-status
readme.

\noindent\writetag{} Source~06 shipped only its \LaTeX{} source, its PDF and
a short plain-text readme. None of them is shipped here (the source is merged
into this report; the archive survives in commit \texttt{fb8414869}); there is
no program to rerun.

\subsection{Finite verification}
The included \texttt{verify.py} uses Python's standard-library
\texttt{fractions.Fraction}. Its output on the supplied version is:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lr@{}}
\toprule
Test family & Cases passed\\
\midrule
Standard division in rational samples of $\R^d\times\Z$ & 7,595\\
Coset-invariant finite-witness tests & 9,000\\
Polynomial amplification tuples & 1,875\\
Lexicographic order triples & 6,000\\
\midrule
Total & 24,470\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The finite-witness tests compare the candidate construction with an
independent oracle on rational lexicographic vector coordinates and
integer intervals. The polynomial tests compute in $\Q[X]$, with
$V=\Q X$ and $u$ a positive rational multiple of $X$; they check finite
injectivity, the highest-coefficient sign rule, and lower-term domination.
All comparisons are exact, with fixed random seeds for the sampled tests.

These checks can catch sign, indexing, and residue mistakes. They cannot
establish any statement involving all real points, arbitrary Borel sets,
local compactness, Haar measure, or infinite cardinalities. The mathematical
proofs, not the tests, carry those conclusions.

python3 verify.py --output verification_results.json
\end{verbatim}
The PDF should then be rendered and visually checked. No external font
files, third-party source PDFs, or unexecuted Lean proof claims are included.

Proper-subfield cover and Puiseux obstruction & Proved here,

The full Hahn coanalytic obstruction is credited to classical descriptive

completion preserves algebraic closedness. Not a new algebraic theorem.\\[.45em]

field cores, prefix stabilization, and the fixed-scale binary obstruction.
The hull construction uses a standard completion mechanism; the local/global

restricted field for clarity.\\[.45em]

sets for Levi-Civita and strengthened Polish hulls,
Theorems~\ref{pma:ser:thm:floorregularity} and~\ref{pma:ser:thm:hullfloor}; not a kernel check.\\[.45em]
Open induction & Classical integer-part implication, proved directly for the
constructed ring; no uncountable arithmetic model is asserted.\\[.45em]
Executable verification & Finite rational arithmetic regression tests only.
They do not verify the set-theoretic or topological quantifiers.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

The logical core can be read as
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \text{Pettis}
 &\Longrightarrow \text{local order bounds}\\
 &\Longrightarrow \text{order-topology refinement}\\
 &\Longrightarrow
 \begin{cases}
  \text{full Hahn classification},\\
  \text{countability of integer parts},\\
  \text{uniqueness of complete order presentations}.
 \end{cases}
 \end{aligned}
\]
The proper-subfield-cover theorem is a separate branch from Pettis and
automatic continuity. Descriptive completeness and real closedness are
independent supplementary branches; neither is needed for the main Hahn
classification.

This pin is a reproducible repository locator, not a claim to have rebuilt
the project at that commit. The main proofs here are self-contained and do
not depend on assuming a repository theorem beyond the explicitly credited
normal-form background. The large formalization ledger could not be fetched
through the available connector; no claim-by-claim audit of that ledger is
represented as completed.

\paragraph{Earlier reports.}
The Library copies of the previous Polish Presburger and Borel-support
manuscripts were checked at the abstract/contribution level to avoid presenting
the same results as new. The present article does not use their proposed
theorems as unproved premises. References~\cite{s09:PriorPresburger,s09:PriorSupport}
are unpublished project manuscripts, not independently validated literature.

\paragraph{Literature scope.}
The primary mathematical sources below were accessed during preparation.
The review was targeted, not exhaustive. The sources establish the classical
inputs and the research connection; the priority of the combined classification
and its refinements remains open to specialist review. In particular, no
absence-of-search-result argument is used as evidence of novelty.

\paragraph{Build and checks.}
The archive contains this self-contained \texttt{article.tex}, the compiled
PDF, \texttt{verify.py}, its machine-readable output
\texttt{verification.json}, a source record, and a build README.
Compile with a standard TeX Live installation using
\begin{verbatim}
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error article.tex
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error article.tex
pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode -halt-on-error article.tex
python3 verify.py --output verification.json
\end{verbatim}
The Python program uses only the standard library. It records deterministic
finite test counts and fails on a violated identity. No numerical tolerance
is used: all tested coefficients and exponents are exact rational numbers.
The PDF was also rendered for layout inspection. Font packages are referenced
by the TeX source; no font files are distributed in the archive.

\noindent\writetag{} In this report the program is
\fn{code/09-hahn-polishability-verify.py}, its recorded output
\fn{data/09-hahn-polishability-verification.json}, the build script
\fn{code/09-hahn-polishability-build.sh}, and the source record
\fn{09-hahn-polishability-SOURCES.md}; they are byte-identical to the delivery
and use the delivery names. The intake reran the program on a copy with
Python~3.14.4: 20{,}116 assertions in 29 families with seed
\texttt{20261003}, equal to the shipped record up to line endings. Source~09's
bibliography and source record cite the surreal README and root README by
unversioned \texttt{blob/main} addresses, beside the pin above; the pinned
versions are
\url{https://github.com/VladimirReshetnikov/ProveIt/blob/4af956fe015e043c5e163ee5b4d2cea23e6157ea/Algebra/SurrealNumbers/README.md}
and the root \fn{README.md} at the same commit. Both files are unchanged
between the pin and the placement commit \texttt{0bd0e5527}.
